\paragraph{12.} (1) Nothing is claimed about the homological dimensions of the full proper-class ring $\Oz$; all
rings are set-sized and all embeddings use finite normal forms. (2) The weak global dimension of $A_d$ is not computed;
only the lower bound $d+1$ is proved. (3) Projective dimensions are given only as bounds (the exact values in rank one
are merge results). (4) Arbitrary non-simplicial cones, arbitrary ideals and all homological localizations of the cores
are not classified. (5) The cores are not integer parts of $\No$, not initial in the simplicity order, not preserved by
all surreal automorphisms and not Hahn rings; the countable-rank ring still consists of finite sums. (6) $\widehat A$ is
the profinite completion, not a Hahn completion or a completion in a surreal-valued topology. (7) The $D+M$ framework
(with the pullback literature, for instance \cite{WKX}), filtered-colimit flatness, coherence criteria and the
idempotent-kernel mechanism of formal \'etaleness are classical and credited; the proposed contributions are the
Boolean $\Tor$ computation, its dimension consequences, the countable-rank realization and the ordered-cone comparison.
(8) No named conjecture is claimed solved; the originality check was targeted, not an exhaustive priority audit.
(9) Not refereed and not checked in Lean. (10) The finite audit uses $\Q\subset\Q(\sqrt2)$ on a five-point grid and proves
nothing over $\Z$ or for all exponents. (11) The Gaussian version uses only the quadratic extension $\SC$; no analytic
continuation and no topology on a proper class is used. (12) ``Nilpotent'' is a test condition on algebras; the cores are
domains.

\paragraph{13.} (1) The theorems are relative to size; nothing is claimed about all maps out of a fixed set-sized Hahn
ring, whose identity is a counterexample ($\Z+X\R[X]$ with evaluation at $1$ is 13's example). (2) No classification of
class quotients, class residue objects or class ideals, and no maximal-ideal theorem or Zorn argument for class rings.
(3) The ordinal result answers only the quotient addendum; the original transcendence-degree question has Wofsey's
answer; 13's priority as a first answer is not established. (4) The integer divisibility of the purely infinite part is
known (L'Innocente--Mantova); equation-only transfer along a retraction, filtered-colimit flatness and the algebra of
idempotent quotient ideals are standard, and the homological and lifting statements are standard consequences, with no
\'etaleness claimed under finite presentation. (5) The fiber theorem (printed in \cite{RepoOdg}) classifies only nonzero
fibers of decomposable polynomials; there is no general classification of fibers, no Thue or Baker finiteness, and no
enumeration of the ordinary solutions of its cubic. (6) The cardinal criterion is a necessary condition, not an existence
theorem for detecting maps. (7) The statement on Hilbert's tenth problem is a transfer of the classical theorem, with no
complexity claim for Gaussian or number-field problems. (8) No claim about Conway's refinement conjecture; no refinement,
primality or unique-factorization property is used. (9) In the two-universe reading ``not generated by a set'' means not
generated by a family from the smaller universe. (10) Unrefereed; no Lean; the finite checks verify identities only; the
literature and repository searches were bounded, and the repository was neither audited nor built.

\paragraph{14.} (1) Arbitrary finitely generated omnific ideals, with generators at several support levels, are not
classified. (2) The rank-one modules with double dual $B$ are not all listed. (3) No coefficient ring other than a
principal ideal domain is treated. (4) The exact weak global dimension of $A_0$ is not claimed. (5) The ideal-class group
under $\odot$ is not a subgroup of $\operatorname{Pic}(A)$, and its identity is $L(\mathfrak o)$, not $A$. (6) Full-class
tensor products are explicit carriers only, and ordinary flat dimensions are asserted only over $A_0$. (7) No novelty is
attached to noncoherence; the $D+M$ context is credited to Dobbs--Papick, and no valuation-ring hypothesis is used.
(8) The number field spanned by a multiplier ring need not be totally real. (9) The unimodular-direction criterion
concerns coordinate representatives, not all points of a projective scheme over a ring, and its example $[1:\sqrt2]$ is
not new. (10) The matrix theorem concerns constant matrices at one common monomial scale. (11) Constant-term arithmetic,
flat idempotent support ideals and cardinal obstructions are credited to companion manuscripts and are not presented as
new. (12) No Conway factorization or refinement claim; no peer review and no new Lean verification; priority is
provisional; the finite checks are supplementary and certify no arbitrary support, class universal property, flatness
statement or Eisenstein criterion in all degrees.

\paragraph{15.} (1) The main theorem is for the full class only; the finite-support ring and fixed Hahn workspaces are
counterexamples. (2) Its local cardinal criterion is sufficient only. (3) No classification of class prime or maximal
ideals; its prime classification carries a set-sized residue hypothesis. (4) The primality of $\omega^{\sqrt2}+\omega+1$ is
imported from L'Innocente--Mantova and is not a new solution of Gonshor's question. (5) No claim about Conway's refinement
problem; the repository \texttt{gaearon/conway-refinement} was noticed but neither built, audited nor relied on. (6) Only
set-sized modules are classified; this is not a Morita statement. (7) Universe parameters must stay separate.
(8) Geometric factorization and the congruence calculations are classical or elementary, the categorical deductions are
not new techniques, and the rotation-group theorem is a precedent, not a dependency. (9) Blocking one extension of the
finite-support augmentation is weaker than the theorem. (10) Embeddings of set-sized rings into $\Oz$ are not excluded,
and $\Oz$ and $\Z$ are not claimed to have the same first-order theory. (11) Candidate originality rests on a bounded
search; no referee; no Lean; the checks cover finite identities only.

\paragraph{16.} (1) Rational-to-polynomial collapse is not new in principle (D-rings: Gunji--McQuillan, as recalled by
Liu; related framework in \cite{CFMT}). (2) Surjectivity rigidity covers one variable and rational functions only;
conjugation, multivariate triangular bijections, class functions, definable maps and formal power series are excluded,
and not every bijection of $\Oz$ is claimed affine. (3) The fresh-scale arguments do not transfer to a fixed Hahn field
without dominant exponents. (4) The certificates are relative to exact coefficient data, zero tests and a certificate of
freshness; they are not a decision procedure for arbitrary surreal inputs. (5) No convergence, analytic continuation,
surreal exponential, Berarducci--Mantova derivation, fine-topological completeness or factorization is used. (6) Newton
interpolation and binomial bases are classical, and the Gaussian test is not claimed minimal. (7) The fiber theorem
classifies the particular fresh fiber, not every fiber. (8) Priority rests on a bounded search; no referee; no Lean; the
finite checks are not proofs.

\paragraph{17.} (1) No Lean formalization and no peer review; no named factorization conjecture is resolved, and no factorization
property is inferred from noncoherence. (2) The kernel theorem classifies constant matrices after one common monomial scaling, not
matrices with several leading scales or positive tails; ideals not of the form $J_g(M)$ are not classified. (3) No upper bound on the
weak global dimension of $R^{11}_\kappa$, no global dimension, and no projective dimension of $\Pi^{11}_\kappa$ or $J_g(M)$ is claimed (the
last two are asked); Dobbs's valuation-pullback theorem does not apply because $K+\Pi^{11}_\kappa$ is not a valuation ring. (4) The
power-growth law counts generators only; the lattices $M^n$ need not stabilize when their ranks do, and it is not a reconstruction
theorem. (5) Over the class ring, $\Hom$ means scalar codes, not a class of class maps; ordinary $\Tor$, flat resolutions and weak
dimensions are taken over set-sized models only, and no full-class $\Tor$ is inferred from the statement on modules killed by the
tail. (6) Neither inversion closure of $R^{11}_\kappa$ nor its equality with a Hahn field is asserted; nothing is said about unrestricted
Hahn series over an arbitrary divisible group, and the coinitiality of the positive cone is not the cofinality of the group. (7) The
appendix proof of the universal theorem is background, not a novelty candidate. (8) The $D+M$ noncoherence mechanism and the relation
module $M^{r-1}$ are classical (Dobbs, Dobbs--Papick); the pullback literature may contain antecedents of the $\Hom$ and tensor formulas;
the audit of the repository and of the literature was targeted, not exhaustive. (9) The rank algorithm assumes an exact description
of the coefficient span, not floating-point data. (10) The checks are finite arithmetic; kernel bases computed over $\Q$ are not
integral lattice bases, and no proper-class argument, regularity statement, Hahn support or homological theorem is checked. (11) A
field $\Frac(D)M$ in the field test does not make $M$ a ring. (12) The support-gap argument does not transfer to unrestricted Hahn series
with real exponents.

\paragraph{18.} (1) The dichotomy is one-variable: $XY$ defeats any bound in several variables, only the linewise statement is proved,
and how the exceptional cosets on different lines fit together is open. (2) The coefficient test is an algebraic certificate relative to
exact coefficient operations, not an algorithm for arbitrary surreal representations. (3) Rational collapse is not new (D-rings:
Gunji--McQuillan, recalled by Liu); the description of the integer-valued polynomials and the matrix and jet reductions are applications,
and the all-size matrix test is a general algebraic observation. (4) Finite testing concerns polynomials of known degree only; no finite
sample tests rational functions, and entire Hahn series and nonstandard degrees are excluded. (5) The Gaussian simplex test is not
claimed minimal; the results of Volkov and Petrov are classical input. (6) Surjections are classified among rational maps of one
variable only, not arbitrary bijections, automorphisms of the surreal field or polynomial automorphisms in several variables;
injectivity alone is weaker. (7) Nothing is claimed about $\No$ or the finite surreals, where the discrete gap fails, and no
factorization conjecture is addressed. (8) The checks cover finite-support models $\Q[H]$ and $\Q(i)[H]$ only and prove neither the cluster
lemma, nor the proper-class reading, nor novelty. (9) Novelty rests on a bounded search; no referee; no Lean. (10) The sharp examples need
an infinite element, and for a general discretely ordered ring finite testability of preservation must not be assumed.

\paragraph{19.} (1) $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$ is not determined: its size, its atoms, whether it is trivial and whether it is a set (20 has since
shown that it is nontrivial, atomless and a proper class, \cref{osq:bb:thm:atoms}; its isomorphism type is still not determined). (2) There is no
membership criterion for $\NN$ by normal forms (20 adds support descent and the one-scale rational case, \cref{osq:bb:thm:descent,osq:bb:thm:rational},
but no general criterion). (3) $\mathsf B_\bullet$ is neither one finite product nor an unrestricted product of copies of
$\overline\Z$. (4) The real-case coefficient embedding depends on the choice of $\mathsf v$; no embedding of $\C$ into $\No$ is asserted. (5) No
B\'ezout property of $\Oz$ or of the normalizations is claimed. (6) The characteristic-$p$ fibres are neither reduced nor fields; the mod-2
splitting is a product of nonreduced rings. (7) Set-sized images with zero divisors are classified only up to the factorization through
$\mathsf B_\bullet$; not all set-sized images are claimed countable; $\mathsf B_\bullet$ is not asserted to be a set (20 has since proved it a proper
class), and no universal object in a category of set-sized rings is claimed. (8) The kernel detection uses an explicit foundation (class theory with global choice, no class
Zorn lemma, no measurable cardinals), and its intersections are pointwise kernel characterizations, not sets of class maps. (9) In the
finite-axiom form, $\upsilon_T$ need not be infinitesimal, non-set-generation needs the full class, the small-image theorem needs a cardinal
condition absent from the axioms, and nothing transfers to a fixed Hahn field, where the support $\{1/n\}$ blocks the monomial proof
without being a counterexample (20's reduced-fibre result, \cref{osq:bb:prop:fixedboolean}, avoids the axioms and decides no radicality). (10) Integrality, absolute integral closure, Boolean powers and the B\'ezout property of $\overline\Z$ are
standard, and the small-image theorem is credited; priority is not established. (11) $\NN$ itself is not absolutely integrally closed,
lifted roots need not stay distinct modulo $\Pi\NN$, and norm-one units of the fibre are not asserted to lift. (12) No named factorization
conjecture is resolved; no referee; no Lean; the checks are finite identities and rational inequalities.

\paragraph{20.} (1) The class isomorphism type of $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$ is not determined: atomlessness and free subalgebras of every set size
do not make the class algebra free, complete or saturated for arbitrary Boolean types, and give no arbitrary infinite joins; no isomorphism
$\mathsf B_\R\cong\mathsf B_\R\times\mathsf B_\R$ is claimed (the proved doubling is $\mathsf B_\C\cong\mathsf B_\R\times\mathsf B_\R$). (2) The
fixed-workspace statements use $\sqrt{\Pi(G)\NN(G)}$; $\Pi(G)\NN(G)$ is not asserted radical in any fixed workspace, no atomlessness of
$\Idem(\mathsf C(G))$ for a fixed $G$ is asserted, and the rank extension needs a new dominant coordinate, which a fixed workspace need not
supply. (3) Support descent is not a decision algorithm; effective membership for general infinite supports is open, and the exact
rational criterion concerns one monomial scale. (4) The matrix corollary computes no projective or global dimension and forms no category of
class-sized modules; the higher projective dimensions of this report (\cref{osq:q:pd}) are not addressed. (5) $\sigma_\pm$ are specializations
of a finite branch algebra, not evaluations of Laurent series at $T=0$ or maps on the Laurent field; $\operatorname{pr}_G$ is linear, not a ring
retraction; no evaluation map on all surreal numbers is used. (6) An idempotent residue does not say that its representative is idempotent;
the quotient is not standard part. (7) The free algebra over an infinite index set is a directed union of finite products, not an
unrestricted product. (8) The foundation is class theory with global choice; no set of all class homomorphisms or prime class ideals is
formed, no set-sized Zorn argument is applied to a proper class, set-indexed families of class maps are single uniform class relations, and
statements about all maps are pointwise. (9) The coefficient copy of $\overline\Z$ is not canonical. (10) Radicality, saturation, the Boolean
power, integral doubling and domain-map detection are 19's and the small-image theorem is this report's, reproved and not claimed; general
Boolean powers, multiquadratic extensions, the determinant trick, coefficient projection as a module map and ultrafilter methods are not
new; it is not asserted that 19 was in the repository at 20's pin. (11) The questions resolved are those of 19, not a named conjecture; no
Gonshor prime-element, refinement or other named factorization problem is solved. (12) The source comparison was targeted; historical
priority is not established. (13) The finite checks verify polynomial identities, branch specializations, finite Boolean tables, finite support
projections and initial Laurent coefficients, not class or set arguments, Hahn support theorems, arbitrary supports, integrality of arbitrary
surreals or novelty. (14) No Lean formalization, no repository build, no peer review; Python and SymPy versions other than those recorded
(3.13.5 and 1.14.0) were not tested.

\paragraph{The merge.} (1) The results marked \merge\ are consequences of the sources' lemmas; they
are no more refereed than the sources and are not formalized. (2) No general law is asserted for the
generator counts. (3) For full-support Hahn rings over a prescribed set group, exact thresholds are computed only in
\cref{osq:ex:lexQQ,osq:ex:countablecoeff}; the general questions of 04 and 10 stay open. (4) The
support-threshold theorem is stated for $k\in\{\R,\C\}$ as in 04 and 11, not for every set field.
(5) No Gaussian analogue of 10's congruence-preservation criterion is given. (6) Nothing is claimed about the
homological dimensions of the class ring $\Oz$; $\pd_{A_d}D$ for $d\ge2$ and the weak global dimensions of the cores remain
undetermined. (7) The finite-support cores of 12 and 14 are outside \cref{osq:thm:universal}: they have set-sized images
detecting the tail. (8) \Cref{osq:hd:prop:pd}(v) needs a countably generated tail; among the five constructions it settles
only $A^{08}_\kappa$ with $\cf\kappa=\aleph_0$. (9) The extensions of 17's results to $\kappa=\aleph_0$ and of its $\Tor$ formula to the cores
say nothing about the class ring's homological dimensions, and $R^{11}_{\aleph_0}$, like the cores, is outside \cref{osq:thm:universal}.
(10) The comparison of Gaussian tests concerns the sizes of two explicit certificates, not the minimal size of a test, and
\cref{osq:nf:rem:candidates} by itself decides nothing about $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$ (its candidates are decided in \cref{osq:bb:prop:candidates}).
(11) \Cref{osq:bb:cor:descent03} is a descent, not a membership test for $\NN_\kappa$, and \cref{osq:bb:prop:candidates} decides the idempotents
$(1+\bar u_H)/2$ only for $H=\iota_b(h)$ with $h\in TK_G[T]$, in particular for positive $H\in\Pi$ supported in one cyclic group; other $H$ are
not decided. (12) \Cref{osq:bb:rem:choice} concerns the splitting results only; the extension of branch maps and 19's detection still use global
choice.

\subsection{Open questions and their status}\label{osq:sub:questions}
The sources pose the following questions, each as a question suggested by the proofs rather than as
an established open problem. Several coincide and are merged.

\begin{question}[Least detecting targets over a fixed exponent group; 03, 04, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 13, 15]\label{osq:q:fixed}
For a set-sized ordered group $\Gamma$, a family of admissible supports and a specified element (or
the whole positive-support ideal), what is the least cardinality of a ring target detecting it, and
which intrinsic support conditions characterize it?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open in general. The field bound (\cref{osq:prop:fieldbound}) is a lower bound that is
exact for the five constructions and for the two examples of \cref{osq:sub:fieldbound}; there it answers
04's ``optimal small targets'' question for its own example, and for 10's question ``when is the
bound attained'' it shows that the bound need not be attained (in the rational example, and for
$A^{03}_{\aleph_1}$ if CH fails). 08's criterion
and 11's coinitiality theorem give sufficient conditions (07's ``sharp set-sized analogues'' and 09's
``quantitative problem'' are the same question). 13's question (how large a target must be to detect a prescribed
series, and whether its divisibility bound is optimal) and 15's ``optimal local cardinal bounds'' are this question as
well: their bounds are the monomial count of \cref{osq:prop:monomialbounds}(ii), which the field bound dominates; the
threshold theorem (\cref{osq:thm:thresholds}) answers them for the five constructions, and \cref{osq:ex:countablecoeff}
shows that the monomial bound need not be attained.

\begin{question}[Cardinal arithmetic without the matching hypothesis; 06]\label{osq:q:kappa}
For $\kappa$ uncountable regular with $\kappa^{<\kappa}>\kappa$, what is the least size of a target
detecting a nonzero element of $\Pi^{06}_\kappa$, and is there an integer part of size exactly $\kappa$
with the same lower bound?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Answered. The least size is $\kappa^{<\kappa}$ in ZFC (\cref{osq:thm:model06}, a merge
result from 06's collision lemma); 08 constructs, for every infinite $\kappa$ in ZFC, an integer part of
size exactly $\kappa$ with threshold $\kappa$ (\cref{osq:thm:model08}), without claiming its fraction field.

\begin{question}[Higher projective dimension; 06]\label{osq:q:pd}
What is $\pd_A\Z$ for the set-sized integer parts, and how does it depend on the cofinality of the
principal-ideal chain?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Partly answered. $\fd_AD=1$ and $\pd_AD\ge2$ are known for all five constructions. If the tail is
countably generated, that is if the principal-ideal chain has countable cofinality, then $\pd_AD=2$
(\cref{osq:hd:prop:pd}(v), a merge result from 12's telescope); among the five constructions this is the case exactly for
$A^{08}_\kappa$ with $\cf\kappa=\aleph_0$. For chains of uncountable cofinality (03, 06, 09, 11, and 08 with
$\cf\kappa>\aleph_0$) the question stays open. For comparison, the ordered-cone cores have $\pd D=2$ and the
coordinate-cone cores $d\le\pd_{A_d}D\le d+1$ (\cref{osq:hd:q:cores}); 17's countable lexicographic model has $\pd D=2$ and
$\pd L(M)=2$ (\cref{osq:rel:rem:alephzero}), and 17 asks the same question for its uncountable models (\cref{osq:rel:q:relations}).

\begin{question}[Nonarithmetic primes and their residue fields; 06, 07]\label{osq:q:primes}
Which prime or maximal class ideals of $\Oz$ have nonzero constant-term image and zero intersection with
$\Z$, and which class fields occur as $\Frac(\Oz/P)$ for primes $P\not\supseteq\Pi$? Can the embedded
field $\FF_a$ distinguish such primes?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open. Necessary conditions: \cref{osq:if:cor:primefield,osq:if:prop:reservoir,osq:if:rem:composition}.

\begin{question}[The ambient binomial quotient; 07]\label{osq:q:binomial}
Which normal-form invariants describe the elements and ideals of $\Qa_a$ beyond the Cantor algebra;
which idempotents lie outside it; how are cyclotomic decompositions at different scales related?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open.

\begin{question}[Residue fields of the set-sized models; 08, 09]\label{osq:q:residue}
Which characteristic-zero fields of the threshold cardinality occur as maximal residue fields of
$A^{08}_\kappa$ and of $A^{09}_{\lambda,D}$, and how many maximal ideals are there?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open; existence, cardinality and at least $\lambda$ nonarithmetic maximal ideals
(for 09) are proved.

\begin{question}[Extra structure on the workspace; 08]\label{osq:q:extra}
Can prescribed residue-field behaviour be combined with closure under a surreal derivation or an
exponential, or with an initial embedding, without destroying the finite-coordinate invariant or the
cardinality?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open.

\begin{question}[Intermediate rings; 04, 11, 15]\label{osq:q:intermediate}
Which rings between the finite-support ring and $D+\Pi_k$ (for instance, given by allowed families of
support order types) still have set-sized reflection $D$? Is enough closure under subordinate division
also necessary?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open; \cref{osq:thm:support} settles the case of cardinal support bounds. 09's question on
support bounds below a regular cardinal is addressed for the groups of 06 and 11
(\cref{osq:sub:model06,osq:sub:model11}), where the support sizes of inverses and quotients are checked.

\begin{question}[Beyond ring observations; 04]\label{osq:q:weaker}
Which weaker multiplicative compatibility conditions still force factorization through the residue, and
which structured families of set-sized local models can retain a specified family of scales?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open.

\begin{question}[Invisible class domains and automorphisms; 10, 11, 15]\label{osq:q:invisible}
What are the ideals, class-valued representations and factorization theory of proper-class quotient
domains invisible to all set-sized rings? How much can an automorphism of $\Oz$ do inside $\Pi$ while
fixing $\ct$?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open.

\begin{question}[Normalization; 03]\label{osq:q:normalization}
Give a membership test for $\NN$ on mixed supports; describe the integral closure over a fixed finitely
generated exponent subgroup; decide the descent $\NN\cap F^{03}_\kappa=\NN_\kappa$; find the algebra
generation number of $\NN_\kappa$ between $\cf\kappa$ and $\kappa^{\aleph_0}$; find a class-sized
replacement for the valuative criterion of integrality; describe the infinite set-sized images of $\NN$.
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Partly answered. For the last clause, 19 classifies the set-sized \emph{domain} images of $\NN$ and $\NN_\C$ (they are
$\overline\Z$ and $\Fpbar$, \cref{osq:nf:thm:domainimages}) and shows that every set-sized image factors through
$\NN/\Pi\NN\cong\overline\Z[\Idem]$ (\cref{osq:nf:cor:small,osq:nf:thm:boolean}); images with zero divisors are not classified. 20 shows that
$\Idem(\NN/\Pi\NN)$ is atomless and a proper class and that no set-indexed family of set-sized images is jointly faithful
(\cref{osq:bb:thm:atoms,osq:bb:thm:noseparating}); its isomorphism type is open (\cref{osq:bb:q:structure}). The descent clause is answered
affirmatively: $\NN\cap F^{03}_\kappa=\NN_\kappa$ (\cref{osq:bb:cor:descent03}, a merge result from 20's support projection). The clause on a
fixed exponent subgroup is partly answered: for every set-sized subgroup $G$, $\NN\cap K_G$ is the integral closure $\NN(G)$ of the omnific
integers supported in $G$, so integrality witnesses at larger scales never help, and for $G=\Z a$ the rational one-scale slice is determined
exactly (\cref{osq:bb:thm:descent,osq:bb:thm:rational}); a description of $\NN(G)$ in general is open. The membership test, the generation
number and the valuative criterion stay open; 19 asks the same membership question, for supports in one finitely generated
exponent subgroup first, allowing integrality witnesses at larger scales, and 20 asks for effective support-local certificates
(\cref{osq:nf:q:fibre}(c)). 04's question on Diophantine fibers belongs to the sibling report
\cite{RepoOdg}, and so does 13's question on fibers of polynomials with nonlinear factors.

\begin{question}[Homological dimensions of the cores; 12, 14, merge]\label{osq:hd:q:cores}
For $d\ge2$, is $\pd_{A_d}D$ equal to $d$ or to $d+1$? What are the weak global dimensions of $A_d$ (at least $d+1$) and of
$A_0=A_1$ (at least $2$)? How do cores over non-simplicial cones, their arbitrary ideals and their homological
localizations behave?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open. In rank one $\pd D=2$, and over every ordered-cone core $\pd D=2$ (\cref{osq:hd:prop:pd}).

\begin{question}[Beyond one coefficient lattice; 14]\label{osq:tn:q:lattices}
Replace the single coefficient lattice by a finite filtration of lattices indexed by support levels and determine which
extension data survive multiplication; characterize all finitely generated rank-one modules with double dual $B$; determine
how coefficient rings that are not principal ideal domains change the tensor kernel.
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open.

\begin{question}[Fresh scales; 16]\label{osq:fs:q:fresh}
(a) Can the factorial-residue Gaussian certificate be replaced by an optimal or near-optimal grid, and how does a regular basis
of $\Int(\Z[i])$ interact with the omnific splitting? (b) Under what hypotheses are the formal inverse branches of a polynomial
map in several variables at independently fresh coordinates classified by finitely many forbidden support blocks (triangular
automorphisms exclude a direct analogue of one-variable surjectivity rigidity)? (c) For a fixed set-sized Hahn subfield and its
nonnegative-support ring, which coefficient subgroups admit enough dominant extensions inside the field to recover the image
gap and the rational certificates?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open; (a) is partly addressed by 18: its Gaussian simplex test is smaller than the factorial-residue certificate for
$d\ge3$, and every ordinary testing set for $\Int(\Z[i])$, for instance the Volkov--Petrov sets of size $O(d)$ in one variable, also tests
$\GOz$ (\cref{osq:or:prop:gausstest}); the optimal size is not determined. For (c), 18's discrete-gap arguments, unlike the fresh-scale
ones, do hold in fixed Hahn workspaces (\cref{osq:or:cor:workspace}).

\begin{question}[Relations of lattice ideals; 17]\label{osq:rel:q:relations}
(a) Classify the kernels of omnific matrices with several monomial scales, and determine which coefficient-lattice data beyond $q$ and
$\ell$ they need. (b) Determine the projective dimensions of $\Pi^{11}_\kappa$ and $J_g(M)$ over $R^{11}_\kappa$, and an upper bound for its weak
global dimension. (c) How much of a finitely generated coefficient algebra do the product lattices, $\Hom$ modules and mixed
multiplication kernels together reconstruct?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open, except (b) for $\kappa=\aleph_0$: $\pd\Pi^{11}_{\aleph_0}=1$ and $\pd J_g(M)=2$ for $r\ge2$ (\cref{osq:rel:rem:alephzero}, a merge
result). For uncountable $\kappa$, (b) is \cref{osq:q:pd} for $R^{11}_{\Gamma_\kappa,<\kappa}$; (a) is also 14's question (\cref{osq:tn:q:lattices}).

\begin{question}[Ordinary-output loci; 18]\label{osq:or:q:outputs}
(a) Which hypotheses on $f\in\Num_n(\Lambda)$ exclude positive-dimensional fibres and make its ordinary-output locus a finite union of
translated ordinary lattices, explaining both the linewise rigidity and the example $XY$? (b) For a polynomial of degree $d$ without a rich
target, which patterns of the numbers $\#\mathcal L_{f,b}\in\{0,\dots,d\}$ occur as $b$ varies? (c) Can Gaussian test points in $\GOz$
that are not ordinary reduce the least number of test points below the ordinary minimum? (d) Can the cluster theorem be formalized for
an abstract discretely ordered ring and specialized through the normal-form bridge?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open. The real analogue of (c) is settled by 16: for $\Oz^n$ no set of omnific points smaller than $|L|$ tests a
lower-set space $V_L$ (\cref{osq:fs:thm:lower}); for $\GOz$ the lower bound $d+1$ from the dimension is all that is known.

\begin{question}[The arithmetic fibre of the normalization; 19]\label{osq:nf:q:fibre}
(a) Determine $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$: is it trivial, does it have atoms, can independent families of nontrivial idempotents be constructed,
and is it a set? (b) For which fixed exponent groups and support classes is the extended ideal radical in the corresponding real
integral closure, and when does real-rooted lifting give a Boolean-power fibre although set-family divisibility fails? (c) Given finitely
many integral elements with monic witnesses, can a common Boolean partition and its algebraic-integer coefficients be extracted by a
concrete symbolic procedure, including the equality tests in the quotient?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Partly answered. (a) is answered by 20: $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$ is nontrivial and atomless, it is a proper class with no
set-sized order-dense subset, the explicit family of \cref{osq:bb:thm:ordinal} is independent and indexed by all ordinals, and free families of
every set size exist over every set-sized subring (\cref{osq:bb:thm:uniform,osq:bb:thm:free,osq:bb:thm:ordinal,osq:bb:thm:atoms}); the same holds for
$\mathsf B_\C$ (\cref{osq:bb:cor:complex}). The earlier reduction of (a) to instances of the membership clause of \cref{osq:q:normalization}
(\cref{osq:nf:rem:candidates}) is superseded: 20 needs no membership test, and the candidates of that remark are nontrivial
(\cref{osq:bb:prop:candidates}, a merge result). What remains of (a) is the isomorphism type (\cref{osq:bb:q:structure}). (b) is partly
answered: for every nonzero set-sized ordered group the reduced fibre $\mathsf C(G)$ is a Boolean power of $\overline\Z$, and one dominant
coordinate adds free Boolean families (\cref{osq:bb:prop:fixedboolean,osq:bb:thm:rank}); whether $\Pi(G)\NN(G)$ is radical, and whether
$\Idem(\mathsf C(G))$ is atomless, in a fixed workspace stays open, and 20 asks the same. (c) is open; 20 asks it for algebraic Laurent
elements given by equations and branch data, with complexity bounds, and settles the one-scale rational case
(\cref{osq:bb:thm:rational}). \Cref{osq:nf:thm:doubling} holds throughout. 19's membership question is merged into \cref{osq:q:normalization}.

\begin{question}[The class Boolean algebra of the fibre; 20]\label{osq:bb:q:structure}
Which further Boolean invariants determine $\Idem(\mathsf B_\R)$? Can its automorphisms, or its possible infinite joins, be characterized
directly in terms of support-local arithmetic?
\end{question}
\emph{Status.} Open. Known: it is atomless, a proper class with no set-sized order-dense subset, and relatively free over every set-sized
subring in every set size (\cref{osq:bb:thm:free,osq:bb:thm:atoms}); this does not make it free, complete or saturated.

\section{Verification record and formalization routes}\label{osq:sec:provenance}

\subsection{Finite checks}\label{osq:sub:checks}
Each manuscript ships a program that checks finite identities with exact arithmetic. They are
kept byte-identical in \texttt{code/} with their recorded outputs in \texttt{data/}; all were rerun
for the four merges on copies (Python~3.14.4, SymPy~1.14.0) and reproduce the recorded outputs, up to line
endings, the recorded Python version and, for 18, the recorded time stamp.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.56\textwidth}rl@{}}
\toprule
Source & What is checked & Checks & Needs\\
\midrule
03 & finite telescopes in a symbolic lattice, the unit series coefficients, lexicographic comparisons & 2{,}362 & stdlib\\
04 & 1{,}950 finite telescopes in $\Q\oplus_{\mathrm{lex}}\Q$ with remainders and positive supports, 600 constant-term and 900 augmentation products, 3 support failures and 3 distinguishing examples & 5{,}406 & stdlib\\
06 & geometric remainders and supports, constant terms, boundary cases, Euler derivation, real complex-structure blocks & 17{,}586 & stdlib\\
07 & weighted geometric identities, binomial remainders, first idempotents, dyadic refinement maps & 621 & SymPy\\
08 & telescopes, constant terms, Hahn Leibniz identities, binomial powers, root corrections & 1{,}277 & stdlib\\
09 & telescopes, truncated comaximality, coefficient maps, finite-support evaluation, Gaussian derivation, gcd identities & 597 & stdlib\\
10 & divided differences, Newton reconstruction, digit indicators and lifts, telescopes, rational differences & 171{,}395 & SymPy\\
11 & finite division identities, positive quotient supports, augmentation and conjugation identities, Gaussian derivation & 1{,}858 & stdlib\\
12 & homology of the Boolean tensor complex for $\Q\subset\Q(\sqrt2)$ on a five-point grid in ranks 1--4, with $d\circ d=0$ (\cref{osq:hd:thm:boolean}) & 780 & SymPy\\
13 & 51 finite geometric remainders and their positivity, a cubic norm determinant, a Laurent norm identity, a rank-deficient point, a constant-term product & 6 groups & stdlib\\
14 & ranks for $\alpha^d=2$, homogenized Rees equations, quadratic torsion generators, $P^2=2\,\Z[\sqrt{10}]$ and the mod-5 obstruction, action associativity and tensor balancing & 2{,}189 & SymPy\\
15 & finite telescopes and divisor truncations, constant-term products, finite-support evaluation, Gaussian matrix evaluation & 820 & stdlib\\
16 & Newton reconstruction, lower-set interpolation matrices, Gaussian counterexample, 150 centered defects, 22 formal inverse branches & 1{,}354 & SymPy\\
17 & coefficient multiplication matrices and kernels (quadratic, cubic, full orders, mixed, independent coefficients), the Pell matrix, fractional-linear identities, descent ranks $q,\ell$ & 347 & SymPy\\
18 & Newton expansions on finite-support $\Q[H]$, $\Q(i)[H]$ models, centering formulas, the composition obstruction, rational differences, Lagrange leading coefficients, nilpotent matrices, Gaussian examples (20 categories) & 499 & SymPy\\
19 & integral idempotent and doubling identities, spectral projectors, the Catalan expansion to degree 13, 216 endpoint signs of the lifting (24 polynomials, degrees 1--8) & 253 & SymPy\\
20 & quadratic identities and branch specializations, simple-zero witnesses for 31 radicand products and all 16 four-branch assignments, initial Laurent coefficients to degree 15, Boolean atoms in 1--6 variables and the symmetric difference, 875 support-projection examples and one failure of multiplicativity & 6{,}674 & SymPy\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

These checks catch sign, index and coefficient errors in finite special cases. None of them
verifies an infinite support, a class-size argument, a cardinal computation, the existence of a
maximal ideal, an $\Ext$ computation, a primality statement, or novelty; those rest on the written
proofs (all sources). The merge results were not machine-checked. For the second merge an independent exact
computation, not shipped, rechecked \cref{osq:hd:thm:boolean} in $1{,}626$ cases (multidegree and subset $F$, including
every proper $F$, over $\Q\subset\Q(\sqrt2)$ in ranks up to $3$) and the formulas of \cref{osq:tn:cor:alldegrees} for
$d\le8$, $n\le12$; like the shipped checks it verifies finite cases only. For the third merge, independent exact computations, not
shipped, rechecked the Pell matrix, the multiplication kernels and self-$\Tor$ endpoints for $r\le5$, the irreducibility of $X^r-(1+i)$ over
$\Q(i)$ for $r\le6$ and five $\mathrm{GL}_2(\Z)$ cases (17); the composition obstruction of \cref{osq:or:thm:composition} in 27 random cases and
the formula for $\eta_f$ (18); the idempotent identities, the Catalan expansion and 36 random cases of the lifting bound (19); and the sizes
of the Gaussian certificates compared in \cref{osq:or:prop:gausstest} for $n\le3$, $d\le5$. For the fourth merge an independent exact
computation, not shipped, rechecked in 18 checks the certificate \eqref{osq:bb:eq:splitter}, the values of $\sigma_\pm$, the many-branch identity
of \cref{osq:bb:thm:rank}, the simple zeros used for the square classes, and the identities behind \cref{osq:bb:prop:candidates}.

\subsection{Formalization routes}\label{osq:sub:formalization}
No statement of this report is formalized, and the repository has no omnific module. The sources
propose compatible routes, recorded here as proposals only. First formalize the scaled-field collision
lemma and the separation lemma, which need no surreals (06, 04, 07). Then the countable telescope in a
set-sized Hahn workspace, with its two support inequalities, proved coefficientwise and not by
convergence of partial sums (04, 06, 07, 08, 10). The set-sized criteria
(\cref{osq:thm:criterion,osq:thm:coinitial}) avoid proper-class syntax and are good first targets (08). The full-class theorem needs an explicit universe interface in which the target and the collision
index are small and the exponent class supplies smaller scales below every small support; a
universe-polymorphic theorem about an arbitrary field does not supply them (04, 08, 10, 11). For the
constructions, the finite-coordinate invariant of 08 must be preserved through field generation,
real closure and truncation, and the module bound at a cardinal must use cyclic quotients or the
direct collision, not an endomorphism ring (08, 06). The homological part comes last, over set-sized
rings only (06). For 07's quotient, the support characterization of $\Oz[\omega^{-a}]$ and the exact
binomial kernel should be kept separate from the finite Chinese-remainder algebra. The repository's
small-normal-form modules (\texttt{SmallNormalForm.lean}, \texttt{SmallNormalFormTruncation.lean},
\texttt{SmallNormalFormEvaluation.lean}, \texttt{SignSequenceValuationBalls.lean}, see
\cite{RepoBridge}) are possible prerequisites (04); they are not a certificate for any result here.
The later manuscripts add three proposals. 12 would define its cores as subrings of a monoid algebra cut out by the
constant-coefficient predicate, with the directed-union equality, exactness of the finite-support simplex complex and the
degreewise classification of \cref{osq:hd:thm:boolean} as the main obligations, and the surreal embedding last. 14 would
start from an abstract domain $A=D\oplus P$ with $P$ a $k$-linear ideal in which every element is a product of two, a
hypothesis the finite-support model satisfies. 16 separates a finite part (simple-root recursion, centering, the leading-term
argument, finite differences), a Hahn part (the block embedding) and an actual-surreal part (a common set-sized exponent
workspace and a fresh exponent). The third batch adds three more. 17 separates a finite layer (saturated lattices, constant kernels,
scalar multipliers and coefficient product matrices over a principal ideal domain), a support layer (the small-family gap and the
union of principal ideals) and a homological layer over the set-sized models only. 18 would formalize the cluster theorem for an
abstract discretely ordered ring and specialize it through the normal-form bridge. 19 would formalize the determinant contraction over
any ring with a retraction, radicality under power divisibility, saturation, the root-forcing estimate, the spectral projectors and the
doubling, and keep the class representation theorem and its ultrafilter foundation separate. 20 proposes its set-sized rank extension
(\cref{osq:bb:thm:rank}) as a first target: Hahn coefficient projection, the finite determinant certificate for radical membership, iterated Hahn
fields and the presentation and specialization of a multiquadratic algebra, all over set-sized ordered groups; the full-surreal theorem needs
in addition the normal-form bridge, a bound above a set of exponents and universe management, and the class-ultrafilter representations
should stay a separate foundational module. None was executed.
